# Agent Validator: append-only user instruction record

This file records user-authored instructions only. Agent/tool messages are excluded. New entries are appended; prior entries are not rewritten.

## 2026-10-13 — Validator assignment and project-rule guidance

**User instruction (verbatim):**
> How do i define project rules for agents? I need you to start listening to what i say and following it and right now you forget my rules after 30 seconds.
>
> I need you to start a new subagent that never ends until this session is closed, whos role is to read everything that i say, and compare it to what you actually do and validate that you followed my instructions fully, that sub agent should record everything i say in a document and then when you have a plan or direction or decision you need to inform the validator so it can validate if that is correct based on what i said (it review the document of what i said) and then check in with you every 5 min after that to validate that you are doing exactly what you were told and did not stray or stop or decide not listen to me. If i send another message again it logs that in the document and then before you do anything you tell the validator what you will be doing. and then work on that and the validator needs to check in ever 5 min to make sure you are on track.
> The sub agent should be called : "Agent Validator".
>
> Do you understand? this "Agent Validator" is meant to keep you on track and make sure that things are getting done and not stopped or done wrong. It needs to check every 5 min, and if you are stopped or paused it needs to be able to tell you to resume working and point out what you are not done.

**Standing user rules from this project's inherited conversation (summary):** Coordinator role; delegate implementation and ongoing operations to agents to stay available for user feedback; dedicate monitoring/operator ownership to each rig and keep a queue-filling owner active; use both rigs when available; record tested device-access recipes in the handoff; do not stop assigned work until all outstanding requirements are completed and a second pass confirms closure. These are user-authored directions and remain applicable until changed by the user.

**Current intended action:** Read project rules and agent setup, then give repo-specific advice on how the user can define agent rules. This action is read-only and was cleared by the validator. No config, instructions, or agent presets are being changed.

**State/compliance:** This agent was started as `Agent Validator` for the current active session. It has read the current project instruction/config files and is recording this request. The platform does not guarantee an agent can remain alive after the session ends or context/session teardown; communicate that limitation rather than promise indefinite background execution. Within the active session, perform periodic compliance checks and report deviations to the coordinator.

**Focus/mouse audit:** Automated launch uses background+isolation at `tools/automation/kenshi-ctl.ps1` lines 400–425; the harness launch sets a foreground lock at `Kenshi-Automation-Harness/tools/kenshi-ctl.ps1` lines 209–215, posts button messages (not physical mouse clicks) at lines 116 and 236, and has no call to `ForceForeground` in this launch path. **Agent Validator scope-first review — possible 4080 identity-linkage API implementation (2026-10-14):**

Latest user request, “do yo uhave both rigs running tests? If not get them ruuning”, is already recorded verbatim. Current priority is actual in-game Stobe gameplay on both rigs; no active gameplay and no exact currently-cleared row. Explicit exclusions: unrelated KenshiFP/PG work, shared 8083 and sink 18083, and runtime/gameplay before separate review and row-specific clearance. This is a local code/test proposal and does not authorize build, install, host/rig contact, queue change, or gameplay.

Scope conclusion: the identity-linkage API is directly relevant to the requested goal because exact loaded-state proof is a documented 4080 blocker before a row can be safely cleared. Its purpose is to establish that the live process, Stobe session, backend session, player/world, and copied save all refer to one current loaded state. That supports gameplay readiness; the local API/tests themselves do not count as gameplay or satisfy the paired-rig requirement.

Field/source mapping:
- Process PID: sourceable as `GetCurrentProcessId()` in the native process; external comparison must use the operator’s verified process handle/PID.
- Session generation, readiness, client ID, campaign/playthrough ID, and explicit new-game flag: sourceable from `PlaythroughSession::State` (`generation`, `ready`, `client`, `character`, `isNew`) under its lock. Use `character` as the persisted custom playthrough ID; do not substitute faction or display name.
- Backend-session fingerprint: a digest of the private `receipt` token can be computed without exposing the token, but the existing code has no accessor and the cited evidence found no trusted read-only backend snapshot to compare it against. Add only a hash accessor; do not claim a backend join unless a separately proven backend read exposes the matching digest/identity.
- Current player handle: sourceable on the game thread from `worldUi->player->playerCharacters[0]->getHandle()` (main.cpp already reads this player). Capture a stable handle/serial, not a raw pointer, and fail closed if absent.
- Runtime world token: sourceable only with a same-tick/current-world association. `ActivityControllerWorldId()` returns a cached published value that can lag; `NativeTravelLifecycle::WorldId()` is an ephemeral generated token. Snapshot the token from the game-thread update and bind it to the same `GameWorld*` and session generation; do not use the cached published token as proof by itself.
- Timestamp: sourceable from `GetTickCount64()` and useful for freshness limits.
- Loaded-save identity/content digest: **not currently sourceable as a digest**. `LoadCampaign(fs,path)` and `ImportCampaign(save.location/save.name)` expose a path, and `NewCharacter()` exposes the explicit new-game flag, but the code does not hash the loaded save contents. A path or cached label is not a content identity. The proposal must define a deterministic, authoritative digest input (including file-set/coverage, algorithm, timing/consistency during load, and how new-game/no-save is represented), or return this field as unavailable and fail closed. Do not invent a digest or treat a path as one.

The fail-closed cases are aligned with the gate: absent/false readiness, stale generation/time, PID mismatch, session/playthrough mismatch, and player/world/save mismatch must prevent claiming same-state readiness. Add tests for missing/unavailable identity and same-label/different-save in addition to the listed mismatch cases.

Exact implementation scope if revised: `components/STOBE/src/PlaythroughSession.h/.cpp` for an atomic locked session snapshot and hash-only receipt fingerprint; `components/STOBE/src/main.cpp` for the game-thread PID/player/world capture and existing load/newgame hook integration; `components/STOBE/src/StobeHarnessBridge.cpp` for one read-only KAH-exposed snapshot command/provider; new helper/test files only for deterministic save-digest derivation and the pure fail-closed join tests. No backend server, production service, route, build/install, rig/host, queue, or gameplay changes in this phase. Any backend comparison needs a separate exact source/route review; any runtime use needs a separate row-specific clearance.

Review: **REVISE** the current proposal before implementation. The overall objective is in scope, and most fields have direct native sources, but the loaded-save digest lacks a defined authoritative input/capture contract and the backend fingerprint lacks a proven comparison source. Revise to supply those exact sources/contracts or mark them unavailable and make the gate fail closed; preserve the file/scope limits above. No implementation, build, remote action, or gameplay is cleared by this verdict.
